, through algorithms uniform over all
\psi
with shape
\Delta
.

These properties are in turn exactly those needed for the inductive argument of §
5
.

4.1.
Infinitesimal and Central Characters

We need some preliminary details on infinitesimal and central characters:

4.1.1.
Infinitesimal characters in the classification

Let
G^{*}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
. Then
G^{*}_{\infty}=U^{*}_{N}(F_{\infty})
for
U^{*}
the quasisplit inner form. The Lie algebra
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\infty}=\mathfrak{gl}_{N}(F_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C})
, so consider an infinitesimal character of
G^{*}
as a semisimple matrix up to conjugacy, or in other words,
an unordered sequence

\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\dotsc,\lambda_{n})

with
\lambda_{j}\in F_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}
. We can further expand out each
\lambda_{j}
as a list of complex numbers
\lambda_{j,v}
for each place
v\in\infty
of
F
(which is necessarily real).

It is also sometimes useful to think of each local component
\lambda_{v}
as the generating function
\sum_{j}X^{\lambda_{j,v}}
. In this way, if each
\tau_{i,v}
has infinitesimal character
\lambda^{(i)}_{v}
, we have infinitesimal character assignment

(4.1.1)

\left(\bigoplus_{i}\tau_{i}[d_{i}]\right)_{v}\mapsto\sum_{i}\lambda^{(i)}_{v}[d_{i}]:=\sum_{i}\lambda^{(i)}_{v}\sum_{l=1}^{d_{i}}X^{\frac{d+1}{2}-l}.

It can be seen from this that the character of
\tau[d]
determines that of
\tau
.

Finally, recall from §
3.1.1
that we call
\lambda
regular integral if it is the infinitesimal character of a finite-dimensional representation. Equivalently, for each
v\in\infty
the
\lambda_{i,v}
are distinct and are all integers if
N
is odd or all half-integers if
N
is even.

4.2.
Definitions

Our notion of shape is built off of the details of how Mok assigns an element of
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(N)
to a parameter.

In
2.2.3
we saw that for each cuspidal parameter
\tau\in\widetilde{\Psi}_{\mathrm{ell}}(N)
with regular integral infinitesimal character
\lambda
at infinity and of parity
\delta=\pm 1
, there exists a unique
H=H(\delta)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
depending only on
\delta
such that
\tau
is a parameter for
H(\delta)
.
Motivated by this, we define:

Definition 4.2.1
.

A
(refined) shape
is a sequence

\Delta=(T_{i},d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})_{i}

up to permutation and where the
T_{i}
and
d_{i}
are positive integers,
\lambda_{i}
is an infinitesimal character of rank
T_{i}
, and
\eta_{i}=\pm 1
.

We say that
\psi\in\Delta
, or that
\psi
has (global) shape
\Delta
, if
\psi
is elliptic and
\psi=\bigoplus_{i}\tau_{i}[d_{i}]
with each
\tau_{i}
of rank
T_{i}
and such that each
\tau_{i}
has infinitesimal character
\lambda_{i}
at infinity and is of parity
\delta_{i}=\eta_{i}
.

We let
\Psi_{\mathrm{ell}}(\Delta)\subseteq\widetilde{\Psi}_{\mathrm{ell}}(N)
be the set of all elliptic, self-dual parameters on
G(N)
of shape
\Delta
.

Note
.

For this work, all shapes will be refined. Beware that this does not match the usage of shape elsewhere in the literature, e.g.
[
MS19
]
.

Note
.

Unitary groups
G
may be realized as twisted endoscopic groups of
\widetilde{G}(N)
in two different ways. The
\eta_{i}
data is a technical necessity to select which of these realizations the shape corresponds to—it is not very important conceptually.

Definition 4.2.2
.

Let
\lambda
be an infinitesimal character of rank
N
and
\eta=\pm 1
. Then
\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta}
is the shape
(N,1,\lambda,\eta)
.

Definition 4.2.3
.

The shape
\Delta
is
integral
if its
total
infinitesimal character as determined by formula (
4.1.1
) is regular integral.

In particular, if
\Delta
is integral, then each of the
\lambda_{i}
must be regular integral, though this isn’t sufficient.

4.3.
Properties

We list the key properties of shapes, and stress one last time that although we drooped the adjective, we refer to refined shapes:

Proposition 4.3.1
.

Let the shape
\Delta
have rank
N
such that all the
\lambda_{i}
are integral. Then there is a group
H(\Delta)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(N)
such that all
\psi\in\Delta
are parameters for
H(\Delta)
.

Proof.

The assignment described in §
2.2.3
only depends on
T_{i}
,
d_{i}
, and
\eta_{i}
.
∎

This gives us two quick corollaries:

Corollary 4.3.2
.

Let
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(N)
. Then

\Psi_{\mathrm{ell}}(G)=\bigsqcup_{\Delta:H(\Delta)=G}\Psi_{\mathrm{ell}}(\Delta).

Proof.

This follows from the above since every parameter has a shape.
∎

Corollary 4.3.3
.

Let
\lambda
be an integral infinitesimal character of rank
N
. Then

H(\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta})=U_{\eta(-1)^{N-1}}(N).

In particular, for every
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
and infinitesimal character
\lambda
of a finite-dimensional representation on
G
, there is
\eta
such that
H(\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta})=G
.

As three more facts, if
\Delta
is a shape:

•

All
\psi\in\Delta
correspond to the same pair
(S^{\natural}_{\psi},s_{\psi})
by formula (
2.4.1
). Call the common values
S^{\natural}_{\Delta}
and
s_{\Delta}
. We can similarly define a common
\mathcal{S}_{\Delta}
.

•

All
\psi\in\Delta
have the same Arthur-
\mathrm{SL}_{2}
, so the Arthur-
\mathrm{SL}_{2}
of
\Delta
is well-defined.

•

The infinitesimal character at infinity of
\psi\in\Delta

is determined by (
4.1.1
).

Finally:

Lemma 4.3.4
.

Let
\Delta
be an integral shape. There exists an AJ-parameter
\psi_{\infty}^{\Delta}
such that for all
\psi\in\Delta
,
\psi_{\infty}=\psi_{\infty}^{\Delta}
.
Furthermore, the induced localization map
S^{\natural}_{\Delta}\to S^{\natural}_{\psi_{\infty}^{\Delta}}
is also determined by
\Delta
.

Proof.

The shape
\Delta
determines the localization
\psi_{\infty}
of any parameter
\psi\in\Delta
: for
\psi_{\infty}\mid_{\mathrm{SL}_{2}}=\oplus_{i}\nu(d_{i})^{T_{i}}
, this is immediate. For the restriction
\psi_{\infty}\mid_{W_{\mathbb{R}}}
, let
\psi=\tau_{i}[d_{i}]
such that each term has infinitesimal character
\lambda_{i}
. Then by a known case of the Ramanujan conjecture as explained in
[
MS19
, Lem 6.1]
, the parameter associated to
\tau_{i,\infty}
is bounded with infinitesimal character
\lambda_{i}
matching that of a finite-dimensional representation, so it is uniquely determined. The parameter
\psi_{\infty}
is determined from this data following the constructions of §
2.3.1
.

By construction, the resulting
\psi_{\infty}
has a regular integral infinitesimal character. Additionally, it maps the Arthur-
\mathrm{SL}_{2}
to a principal
\mathrm{SL}_{2}
of the Levi
\widehat{L}=\prod_{i}GL_{d_{i}}^{T_{i}}\subset\widehat{G}
, with
\widehat{L}=Z_{\widehat{G}}(\psi_{\infty}(W_{\mathbb{C}}))
following the assumption that the total infinitesimal character is regular. Then
\psi_{\infty}(W_{\mathbb{C}})\subset Z(\widehat{L})
, and since
\psi_{\infty}
was built from parameters of discrete series,
\psi_{\infty}\mid_{W_{\mathbb{C}}}
factors through
z\mapsto\frac{z}{\bar{z}}
and
W_{\mathbb{R}}
acts on
Z(\widehat{L})
by inversion. Thus the identity component of
Z(\widehat{L})^{W_{\mathbb{R}}}
is trivial, and
\psi_{\infty}
is an AJ-parameter following Definition
3.2.1
.

For the last statement, the map
I^{+}_{\infty}\to I^{+}
described after formula (
3.2.1
) can be seen to depend only on
\Delta
. This determines the localization map.
∎

5.
The Trace Formula with Fixed Shape

Let
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(N)
(the case of
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
suffices for us). If
\Delta
is a shape such that
H(\Delta)=G
, define

S^{G}_{\Delta}:=\sum_{\psi\in\Delta}S^{G}_{\psi}.

This
S^{G}_{\Delta}
is the main technical building block in our applications, and understanding it is the key step in our argument.

For an infinitesimal character
\lambda
, let
\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}
be the Euler-Poincaré function introduced in §
3.3
.
The overarching goal for this section is:

Goal
.

Let
\lambda
be the infinitesimal character of a finite-dimensional representation of
G_{\infty}
. Understand
S^{G}_{\Delta}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty})
as a linear combination of terms
I^{H}_{\mathrm{disc}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda^{\prime}}(f^{\infty})^{\prime})
on other groups
H
.

The terms
I^{H}_{\mathrm{disc}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda^{\prime}}(f^{\infty})^{\prime})
are well-understood—they were given an explicit formula in
[
Art89a
]
, which was in turn studied in great detail and bounded in
[
ST16
]
. Achieving the goal would therefore give fine control over
S_{\Delta}
.

Taïbi in
[
Taï17
]
gave such a description in the level-
1
case. This work is basically extending his method as much as possible to deeper levels.

5.1.
Overall Strategy

We build up
S^{G}_{\Delta}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty})
from
I^{H}_{\mathrm{disc}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda^{\prime}}(f^{\infty})^{\prime})
terms in stages:

(1)

Switch from
I
to
S
to allow for various transfers in the endoscopic classification using that
S^{H}_{\mathrm{disc}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty})
can be expanded as a linear combination of
I^{H^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{disc}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda^{\prime}}(f^{\infty})^{\prime})
terms through “hyperendoscopy” as in
[
Fer07
]
.

(2)

Define a sum of traces on each
\widetilde{G}(M)
called
S^{M}(\lambda,\eta,f^{\infty})
satisfying that if
H=H(\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta})
,
S^{M}(\lambda,\eta,f^{\infty})=S^{H}_{\mathrm{disc}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(f^{\infty})^{H})
. The use of traces on
\widetilde{G}(M)
allows inducting by decomposing
\Delta
down to its simple constituents.

(3)

Define sub-sums
S^{N}_{\Delta}(f^{\infty})
of
S^{N}(\lambda,\eta,f^{\infty})
corresponding to individual shapes. All the
S^{N}_{\Delta}(f^{\infty})
can be computed from terms
S^{M}(\lambda,\eta,(f^{\infty})^{\prime})
by an induction in two steps:

•

S^{N}_{\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta}}(f^{\infty})=S^{N}(\lambda,\eta,f^{\infty})-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}H(\Delta)=H(\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta})\\
\text{Inf. Char}(\Delta)=\lambda\\
\Delta\neq\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta}\end{subarray}}S^{N}_{\Delta}(f^{\infty}).

When
N=1
, the sum of the “correction terms” vanishes—this is the base case of our induction.

•

If
\Delta=(T_{i},d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})_{i}
with infinitesimal character
\lambda
, express
S_{\Delta}^{N}(f^{\infty})
in terms of the
S_{\Sigma_{\lambda_{i},\eta_{i}}}^{T_{i}}
. This is the hardest part of the argument and the partial results needed for our applications will be postponed to Sections
7
and
8
.

(4)

Transfer back to the classical group—
S^{G}_{\Delta}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty})
can be written as
S^{N(G)}_{\Delta}(f^{\infty}_{1})
as long as we can find
(f^{\infty}_{1})^{G}=f^{\infty}
.

In our actual argument, steps 2 and 3 will not be so clearly separated. We will use step 2’s Proposition
5.3.1
to switch freely between the perspective of traces on
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(N)
and traces on
\widetilde{G}(N)
as convenient to perform the induction of step 3.

Since there is a lot of notation introduced in this section, we summarize all the parts of the trace formula defined:

•

I^{G}_{\Delta},I^{G}_{\psi}
are the pieces of the spectral decomposition of Arthur’s
I^{G}_{\mathrm{disc}}
on some
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(N)
corresponding to a shape
\Delta
or individual parameter
\psi
.

•

S^{G}_{\Delta},S^{G}_{\psi}
are the same for Arthur’s
S^{G}_{\mathrm{disc}}
.

5.2.
Step 1: Understanding
S^{H}

The
S^{H}
terms can be understood through the hyperendoscopy formula of
[
Fer07
]
. We use notation from
[
Dal22
]
, although the full generality there isn’t necessary since the computation of the endoscopic data of classical groups in
[
Wal10
]
shows that we will never have to take a
z
-extension.

Theorem 5.2.1

(Ferrari’s Hyperendoscopy Formula)
.

Let
\mathcal{HE}_{\mathrm{ell}}(H)
be the set of non-trivial elliptic hyperendoscopic paths of
H
as in
[
Dal22
, §4]
. Then,

S^{H}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty})=I^{H}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty})+\sum_{\mathcal{H}\in\mathcal{HE}_{\mathrm{ell}}(H)}\iota(G,\mathcal{H})I^{\mathcal{H}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}^{\mathcal{H}}(f^{\infty})^{\mathcal{H}})

for constants
\iota(G,\mathcal{H})
and transfers
\star^{\mathcal{H}}
defined there.

Proof.

See
[
Dal22
, Thm 4.2.3]
, and note that using
\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}
at infinity lets us elide the distinction between trace formulas and their discrete parts.
∎

Ferrari gives an explicit formula to write the transfers
\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}^{H}
as linear combinations of Euler-Poincaré functions. See
[
Dal22
, §5.1]
for an English-language presentation though beware that there is a
\rho
-shift between the parameterization
\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}
used here and the parameterization
\eta_{\lambda}
used there.

5.3.
Step 2: Understanding
S^{M}

Fix an infinitesimal character
\lambda
of rank
M
. Using Proposition
4.3.1
, define:

\Psi_{\mathrm{ell}}(\lambda,\eta)=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Delta:H(\Delta)=H(\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta})\\
\text{Inf. Char}(\Delta)=\lambda\end{subarray}}\Psi_{\mathrm{ell}}(\Delta)

and define pieces of the spectral expansion:

(5.3.1)

\displaystyle S^{M}(\lambda,\eta,f^{\infty})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\Delta:H(\Delta)=H(\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta})}S^{M}_{\Delta}(f^{\infty})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{\mathrm{ell}}(\lambda,\eta)}S^{M}_{\psi}(f^{\infty})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{\mathrm{ell}}(\lambda,\eta)}\epsilon^{H}_{\psi}(s^{H}_{\psi})m_{\psi}|\mathcal{S}_{\psi}|^{-1}\tr_{\widetilde{\pi}_{\psi}^{\infty}}(f^{\infty}).

Here,
\pi_{\psi}
is the automorphic representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{M}(\mathbb{A}_{E})
corresponding to
\psi
as in §
2.2.1
and
\widetilde{\pi}_{\psi}
is its extension to
\widetilde{G}(M)
as in §
2.2.2
.

Proposition 5.3.1
.

Let
H=H(\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta})
which is necessarily in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{sim}}(M)
. Then for a test function
f^{\infty}
at the finite places,

S^{M}(\lambda,\eta,f^{\infty})=S^{H}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(f^{\infty})^{H}).

Proof.

Fix
\psi\in\Psi_{\mathrm{ell}}(H)
with infinitesimal character
\lambda
which is necessarily elliptic. Then the stable multiplicity formula
2.6.3
shows that

S^{H}_{\psi}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(f^{\infty})^{H})=\epsilon_{\psi}(s_{\psi})|\mathcal{S}_{\psi}|^{-1}\left(\sum_{\pi_{\infty}\in\Pi_{\psi_{\infty}}}\eta^{\psi_{\infty}}_{\pi_{\infty}}(s_{\psi})\tr_{\pi_{\infty}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda})\right)\\
\left(\sum_{\pi\in\Pi_{\psi^{\infty}}}\eta^{\psi^{\infty}}_{\pi^{\infty}}(s_{\psi})\tr_{\pi^{\infty}}((f^{\infty})^{H})\right).

Corollary
3.4.4
then gives for infinitesimal character
\lambda
:

\sum_{\pi_{\infty}\in\Pi_{\psi_{\infty}}}\eta^{\psi_{\infty}}_{\pi_{\infty}}(s_{\psi})\tr_{\pi_{\infty}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda})=1.

Furthermore, multiplying together the twisted local character relation
2.5.3
over all finite places shows that:

\sum_{\pi\in\Pi_{\psi^{\infty}}}\eta^{\psi^{\infty}}_{\pi^{\infty}}(s_{\psi})\tr_{\pi^{\infty}}((f^{\infty})^{H})=\tr_{\widetilde{\pi}_{\psi}^{\infty}}(f^{\infty}).

In total
S^{H}_{\psi}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(f^{\infty})^{H})=S_{\psi}^{M}(\lambda,\eta,f^{\infty})
.

Since
S_{\psi}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(f^{\infty})^{H})=0
for all
\psi
with infinitesimal character not equal to
\lambda
, summing over
\Psi_{\mathrm{ell}}(\lambda,\eta)
and using Corollary
4.3.2
gives that

S^{M}(\lambda,\eta,f^{\infty})=\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{\mathrm{ell}}(H)}S^{H}_{\psi}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(f^{\infty})^{H}).

By
[
Art89a
, (3.9)]
and equation (
2.6.1
), we know that

I^{H}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(f^{\infty})^{H})=R^{H}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(f^{\infty})^{H})=\sum_{\psi\in\Psi_{\mathrm{ell}}(H)}I^{H}_{\psi}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(f^{\infty})^{H}).

By a hyperendoscopy argument using the expansion in Theorem
2.6.5
, the same sum expansion holds for
S^{H}
. Therefore, we can conclude that

S^{M}(\lambda,\eta,f^{\infty})=S^{H}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(f^{\infty})^{H}).

This finishes the argument.
∎

5.4.
Step 3: The Induction

We give a heuristic overview to keep in mind for understanding step 3. All precise results will be postponed to §§
7
,
8
.

Recall the decomposition

S^{N}(\lambda,\eta,f^{\infty})=\sum_{\Delta:H(\Delta)=H(\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta})}S^{N}_{\Delta}(f^{\infty})

which we were using for the induction in step 3. We now want to understand
S_{\Delta}
in terms of smaller groups.

For the sake of heuristic understanding, we will instead consider the simpler

S^{|H(\Delta)|}_{\Delta}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(f^{\infty})^{H(\Delta)})=S^{|N|}_{\Delta}(f^{\infty}):=\sum_{\psi\in\Delta}m_{\psi}|S_{\psi}|^{-1}\tr_{\widetilde{\pi}_{\psi}^{\infty}}(f^{\infty})

without the
\epsilon
-sign. Consider
\psi=\tau_{1}[d_{1}]\oplus\cdots\oplus\tau_{k}[d_{k}]\in\Delta
. Motivated by the way
\widetilde{\pi}_{\psi}
is defined through parabolically inducing determinant twists of the
\tau_{i}
, assume we could define a “generalized constant term” map

f^{\infty}\mapsto(f^{\infty}_{i})_{\Delta,i}

such that for all
\psi\in\Delta

\tr_{\widetilde{\pi}_{\psi}^{\infty}}(f^{\infty})=\prod_{i}\tr_{\widetilde{\pi}_{\tau_{i}}^{\infty}}(f^{\infty}_{\Delta,i}).

Because the infinitesimal character of
\Delta
is regular and therefore disallows repeated
\tau_{i}
factors, the possible (elliptic)
\psi\in\Delta
are exactly the
\tau_{1}[d_{1}]\oplus\cdots\oplus\tau_{k}[d_{k}]
for all choices of
\tau_{i}\in\Sigma^{T_{i}}_{\lambda_{i},\eta_{i}}
. Therefore we get a heuristic factorization

(5.4.1)

S^{|N|}_{\Delta}(f^{\infty})=C_{\Delta}\prod_{i}S^{|T_{i}|}_{\Sigma_{\lambda_{i},\eta_{i}}}(f^{\infty}_{\Delta,i}),

for
C_{\Delta}
a constant depending on the various
S_{\psi}
,
S_{\tau_{i}}
, and
m_{\psi}
’s that only depend on
\Delta
.

Obviously, we cannot simply ignore the
\epsilon
-sign and we do not have a actual definition of this generalized constant term. In fact, the definition of this generalized constant term would allow us to define the long-desired “stable transfer” between
G
and its endoscopic groups, so it is likely very difficult.

However, §§
7
,
8
will discuss enough partial results that an application to limit multiplicities at specifically split level can be completed.

5.5.
Step 4: Understanding
S^{G}_{\Delta}

This step comes from a corollary to the arguments in step 2:

Corollary 5.5.1
.

Fix a shape
\Delta
of rank
N
and let
G=H(\Delta)
as in Proposition
4.3.1
. Then for any test function
f^{\infty}
on
G(\mathbb{A}^{\infty})
, there is
f^{\infty}_{1}
on
\widetilde{G}(N)^{\infty}
such that
(f^{\infty}_{1})^{G}=f^{\infty}
. Furthermore,

S_{\Delta}^{G}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty})=S^{N}_{\Delta}(f^{\infty}_{1}).

Proof.

The existence of
f^{\infty}_{1}
comes from
[
Mok15
, Prop 3.1.1(b)]
. Then, arguing as in
5.3.1
gives
S^{G}_{\psi}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(f^{\infty}_{1})^{G})=S_{\psi}^{N}(f_{1}^{\infty})
for any
\psi\in\Delta
. Summing over all
\psi\in\Delta
produces the result.
∎

6.
Local Transfer

The argument of §
5.4
requires constructing certain transfers of functions. We are unable to do this in full generality—this section focuses on either constructing various special cases or approximate versions satisfying good enough bounds. The last
Subsection
6.4
will state the full desired conjectures.

We will heavily use the shorthand from Section
1.6.2
throughout. If
G_{v}
is an unramified group over
F_{v}
then
K_{v}:=K^{G}_{v}
will be a hyperspecial subgroup for
G_{v}
.

6.1.
Split Places

We first discuss some results that only hold at split places. Extending Lemmas
6.1.2
and
6.1.3
to non-split places would similarly extend Theorems
11.4.1
and
11.4.2
.

Fix
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
. Let
v
be a finite place of
F
which splits in
E
, so that
E\otimes_{F}F_{v}\simeq F_{v}\times F_{v}
with
\sigma
permuting the two factors. Then
G(N)_{v}\simeq GL_{N}(F_{v})\times GL_{N}(F_{v})
and the action of
\theta_{N}
on
G(N)_{v}
and
{}^{L}\!{G}(N)_{v}
becomes

(g_{1},g_{2})\mapsto(\Ad(J_{N})g_{2}^{-t},\Ad(J_{N})g_{1}^{-t}),

where
{}^{L}\!{G}_{v}\simeq GL_{N}
embedded in
{}^{L}\!{G}(N)_{v}
as the fixed points of
\theta
(note that both the
\pm
embeddings of
[
Mok15
, (2.1.9)]
are the same in this case).

We next outline how Arthur’s local classification for
G_{v}
as a element of
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(N)
agrees with that
coming from the isomorphism
G_{v}\simeq\mathrm{GL}_{N}(F_{v})
:

Lemma 6.1.1
.

Let
v
be a place of
F
which splits in
E
and denote
E\otimes_{F}F_{v}
by
E_{v}
. Let
\pi_{v}
be the irreducible conjugate self-dual representation of
G(N)(E_{v})\simeq GL_{N}(F_{v})\times GL_{N}(F_{v})
coming from the Arthur parameter
\psi_{v}
. Then:

(1)

\pi_{v}
is of the form
\pi_{v}^{0}\otimes(\pi_{v}^{0})^{\vee}
for some irrep
\pi_{v}^{0}
of
\mathrm{GL}_{N}(F_{v})
.

(2)

The canonical extension of
\pi_{v}
to
\widetilde{G}(N)
as in
[
Art13
, §2.2]
has
\theta
acting on
\pi_{v}^{0}\otimes(\pi_{v}^{0})^{\vee}
through
x\otimes y\mapsto y\otimes x
.

(3)

For
\tilde{f}_{v}=(f^{1}_{v},f^{2}_{v})\in\mathcal{H}(G(N)(E_{v})\rtimes\theta)
, we can choose transfer
\tilde{f}_{v}^{G}=f^{1}_{v}\star{}^{\theta}\!f^{2}_{v}
where we define
{}^{\theta}\!f_{v}(g)=f_{v}(\Ad(J_{n})g^{-t})
.

(4)

For
\tilde{f}_{v}=(f^{1}_{v},f^{2}_{v})\in\mathcal{H}(G(N)(E_{v})\rtimes\theta)
,
\tr_{\tilde{\pi}_{v}}(\tilde{f}_{v})=\tr_{\pi^{0}_{v}}(f^{1}_{v}\star{}^{\theta}\!f^{2}_{v})

(5)

\tr_{\psi_{v}}(f_{v})=\tr_{\pi^{0}_{v}}(f_{v})
.

Proof.

Statement (1) follows from the description of irreps of

G_{1}\times G_{2}
and self-duality.

For (2), first assume
\pi_{v}
and therefore
\pi_{v}^{0}
is tempered. A Whittaker functional on
\pi_{v}
is a product of a pair of functionals on
\pi_{v}^{0}
and
(\pi_{v}^{0})^{\vee}
. This product is preserved by the claimed
\theta
since the space of Whittaker functionals is one dimensional. On the other hand, if
\pi
isn’t tempered, the statement can be checked by the construction of
\theta
through parabolic induction. This gives the second statement in all cases.

Statement (3) is a special case of Corollary 1.1.6 in
[
KMSW14
]
.

For (4),
\tilde{f}_{v}(x\otimes y)=(f_{v}^{1}x\otimes{}^{\theta}\!f_{v}^{2}y)
. By admissibility of
\pi_{v}
and smoothness of the
f_{v}^{i}
, we can compute the trace by choosing a basis for the finite-dimensional vector space
V=(\pi_{v})^{U}
for some open compact
U
. The result follows from computing the action on the standard induced bases for
\Sym^{2}V\oplus\wedge^{2}V=V\otimes V
.

Finally, (5) follows from (3), (4), and the endoscopic character relation after choosing
f^{1}_{v}
and
{}^{\theta}\!f^{2}_{v}
such that
f^{1}_{v}\star{}^{\theta}\!f^{2}_{v}=f_{v}
.
∎

We derive two consquences of Lemma
6.1.1
:

Lemma 6.1.2
.

Let
v
be a place that splits in
E
and
\psi_{v}=\psi_{1,v}\oplus\psi_{2,v}
be an Arthur parameter for
U_{E/F}(N)(F_{v})\simeq\mathrm{GL}_{N}(F_{v})
. Then
\psi
factors through (the
L
-dual of) a Levi subgroup
M=\mathrm{GL}_{N_{1}}(F_{v})\times\mathrm{GL}_{N_{2}}(F_{v})
and

\tr_{\psi_{v}}f=\prodf_{i=1,2}\tr_{\psi_{i,v}}f_{M,i},

where
f_{M,1}
and
f_{M,2}
are the factors of the constant term map to
M
.

Proof.

The assumptions imply that
\tilde{\pi}_{\psi_{v}}
is a parabolic induction of
\tilde{\pi}_{\psi_{1,v}}\otimes\tilde{\pi}_{\psi_{2,v}}
, so in the notation of Lemma
6.1.1
(1),
\pi_{\psi_{v}}^{0}
is a parabolic induction of
\pi^{0}_{\psi_{1,v}}\otimes\pi^{0}_{\psi_{2,v}}
. The result follows from
6.1.1
(5).
∎

Lemma 6.1.3
.

Let
v
be a place that splits in
E
and
\psi_{v}=\psi_{1,v}[d]
be an Arthur parameter for
U_{E/F}(N)(F_{v})\simeq\mathrm{GL}_{N}(F_{v})
. Then
\psi|_{W_{F}}
factors through (the
L
-dual of) a Levi subgroup
M=(\mathrm{GL}_{N_{1}})^{d}
.
Furthermore, for all test functions
f
satisfying:

•

f
is supported on the kernel of
|\det|_{v}
,

•

for all unirreps
\pi_{v}
of
\mathrm{GL}_{N}(F_{v})
,
tr_{\pi_{v}}(f)\geq 0
,

we have

\tr_{\psi_{v}}f\leq(\tr_{\psi_{1,v}}f_{M,1})^{d\oplus},

where
f_{M,1}
is the constant term to
M
restricted to the first factor.

Proof.

This is a similar argument to
6.1.2
using that
\tilde{\pi}_{\psi_{v}}
is a summand in the Grothendieck group of the parabolic induction of

\tilde{\pi}_{\psi_{1,v}}|\det|^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\tilde{\pi}_{\psi_{1,v}}|\det|^{\frac{1-d}{2}}.

We may ignore the determinant factors by the support condition. The inequality comes from the positivity condition applied to traces against the other summands in the Grothendieck group expansion.
∎

6.2.
Unramified Places

We next discuss the unramified places. Our results rely on the fact that unramified transfer through any
L
-map can be made very explicit via the Satake parameters.

6.2.1.
Basic transfer result

First we recall an “Arthur packet fundamental lemma” that was the key result making the strategy of §
5
work at level-
1
in
[
Taï17
]
:

Lemma 6.2.1

(Fundamental Lemma for
A
-packets)
.

Let
v
be a place unramified in
E
and

\psi_{v}=\bigoplus_{i}\tau_{i,v}[d_{i}]

be an Arthur parameter for
U_{E/F}(N)(F_{v})
. Then

\tr_{\psi_{v}}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K_{v}}=\prod_{i}\tr_{\psi_{i,v}}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K_{i,v}}

for appropriately chosen hyperspecial subgroups
K_{\star,v}
in appropriate
U_{E/F}(N_{\star})(F_{v})
.

Proof.

This follows from
[
Taï17
, Lem 4.1.1]
since
\psi_{v}
is unramified if and only if each of the
\tau_{i,v}
is. Then, just apply that for any
\pi_{v}
,
\tr_{\pi_{v}}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K_{i,v}}=\mathbf{1}_{\pi_{v}\text{ is unram.}}
.

The
K_{\star,v}
are chosen as in the fundamental lemma according to a choice of Whittaker datum.
∎

Our eventual goal is to prove bounds in the style of
[
ST16
]
, so we need a more general statement for any element of
\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{ur}}(G_{v})
.

6.2.2.
Truncated Hecke algebras

We first recall the notion of a truncated Hecke algebra from
[
ST16
]
. The elements
\tau^{G}_{\lambda}=\mathbf{1}_{K_{v}\lambda(\varpi)K_{v}}
for a chosen unformizer
\varpi
and
\lambda\in X_{*}(A)^{+}
generate
\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{ur}}(G_{v})
. Pick a basis
\mathcal{B}
for
X_{*}(A)
and define the norm

\|\lambda\|_{\mathcal{B}}=\max_{\omega\in\Omega}(\text{biggest }\mathcal{B}\text{-coordinate of }\omega\lambda).

For
\lambda\in X_{*}(A)
, define the truncated Hecke algebra

\mathscr{H}(G,K)^{\leq\kappa,\mathcal{B}}=\langle\tau^{G}_{\lambda}:\|\lambda\|_{\mathcal{B}}\leq\kappa\rangle.

It turns out (see
[
ST16
, §2]
) that for any two
\mathcal{B},\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
,
\|\lambda\|_{\mathcal{B}}
and
\|\lambda\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
are proportional. All the bounds we use will depend on
\kappa
only up to an unspecified constant. Therefore we can suppress the
\mathcal{B}
.

6.2.3.
Basis of characters

Recall that the Satake transform gives an isomorphism

\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{ur}}(G_{v})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}[X_{*}(A)]^{\Omega_{F}},

where
A
is a maximally split maximal torus of
G_{v}
in good position with respect to
K_{v}
. The right side of this isomorphism has a basis
\chi_{\lambda}
of trace characters of finite-dimensional representations
\lambda
of the twisted group
\widehat{G}\rtimes\mathrm{Frob}_{v}
.

Any unramified parameter
\psi_{v}
determines an unramified
L
-parameter which determines a Satake parameter: a semisimple conjugacy class
\sigma_{\psi_{v}}\in\widehat{G}\rtimes\mathrm{Frob}_{v}
. Because of
[
Taï17
, Lem 4.1.1]
, this satisfies that

(6.2.1)

\tr_{\psi_{v}}\chi_{\lambda}=\tr_{\lambda}(\sigma_{\psi_{v}}).

See
[
ST16
, §2.2]
for more detail.

The consistency of unramified packets constructed by Arthur/Mok and the Satake isomorphism is implicit in the isolation of the
\psi
-part
I_{\psi}
of
I_{\mathrm{disc}}
—see §3.3 in
[
Art13
]
for example. It depends on the full fundamental lemma for all spherical functions.

6.2.4.
General unramified transfer

Let
v
be a place unramified in
E/F
and

\psi_{v}=\bigoplus_{i}\tau_{i,v}[d_{i}]\in\Delta=(t_{i},d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})_{i}

be an Arthur parameter for
U_{E/F}(N)(F_{v})
. Let each
\tau_{i,v}
be a parameter for
U(t_{i})
. There is an associated embedding

(6.2.2)

\iota_{\Delta}:\mathbf{H}:={}^{L}\!{H}_{v}\times\prod_{i}{}^{L}\!{\mathrm{GL}}_{d_{i}}:=\prod_{i}{}^{L}\!{(}U(t_{i})\times\mathrm{GL}_{d_{i}})\hookrightarrow{}^{L}\!{G}_{v}.

Let
\psi^{I}_{n}
be the parameter of the trivial representation on
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
. We can write the Langlands parameter
\varphi_{\psi_{v}}
corresponding to
\psi_{v}
as the pushforward of

\prod_{i}\tau_{i,v}\times\psi^{I}_{d_{i}}.

This gives the map on Satake parameters

(6.2.3)

\sigma_{\psi_{v}}=\mathcal{S}_{\Delta}((\sigma_{\tau_{i,v}})_{i}):=\iota_{\Delta}\left(\prod_{i}\sigma_{\tau_{i,v}}\times\sigma^{I}_{d_{i}}\right),

for
\sigma^{I}_{d_{i}}
the Satake parameter of the
d_{i}
-dimensional trivial representation.

Restricting
\iota_{\Delta}
to
\star\rtimes\mathrm{Frob}
cosets determines an unramified transfer map:

\mathcal{T}_{\Delta}:\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{ur}}(G_{v})\to\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{ur}}(H_{v}):\chi_{\lambda}\mapsto\chi_{\lambda}\circ\mathcal{S}_{\Delta}.

Equation (
6.2.1
) then gives:

Lemma 6.2.2
.

With notation as above, let
f\in\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{ur}}(G_{v})
. Then

\tr_{\psi_{v}}(f)=\prodf_{i}\tr_{\tau_{i,v}}(\mathcal{T}_{\Delta,i}f),

where the
\mathcal{T}_{\Delta,i}
are the factors of
\mathcal{T}_{\Delta}f
at each
U(N_{i})
.

The next lemma gives some control over the size of the
\mathcal{T}_{\Delta,i}f
.

Lemma 6.2.3
.

Let
f\in\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{ur}}(G_{v})^{\leq\kappa}
with
\|f\|_{\infty}\leq 1
. Then
\mathcal{T}_{\Delta}f\in\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{ur}}(H_{v})^{\leq\kappa}
and
\|\mathcal{T}_{\Delta}f\|_{\infty}=O(q_{v}^{D\kappa}\kappa^{E})
for some constants
D
and
E
that only depend on
G
and
\Delta
.

Proof.

This is a slightly more complicated version of the argument of Lemma 5.5.4 in
[
Dal22
]
. There is an additional step from bounding the trace of the Satake parameter of the trivial representation against the finite-dimensional irreps of
{}^{L}\!{\mathrm{GL}}_{d_{i}}
that appear as factors in restrictions from
{}^{L}\!{G}_{v}
to
\mathbf{H}
. This can be seen to be
O(q_{v}^{D^{\prime}\kappa})
for some
D^{\prime}
by the Weyl character formula.
∎

We similarly define a partial unramified transfer map
\mathcal{T}_{\bar{\Delta}}
such that

\tr_{\psi_{v}}(f)=\prodf_{i}\tr_{\tau_{i,v}[d_{i}]}(\mathcal{T}_{\bar{\Delta},i}f).

By the same arguments, this map satisfies Lemma
6.2.3
. We will often suppress
\Delta
or
\bar{\Delta}
in notation—which version of
\mathcal{T}
we are using should always be clear by the context of what the
\mathcal{T}_{i}
’s have image in. We note for intuition that
\mathcal{T}_{\bar{\Delta}}
can be thought of as hyperendoscopic transfer as in Theorem
5.2.1
, see
[
Dal22
, §5.5]
for details.

Finally, if
\Delta
is a simple shape of the form
(1,d,\lambda,\eta)
, then for
\psi\in\Delta
the possible
\sigma_{\psi_{v}}
from (
6.2.3
) are all Satake parameters of characters. In addition,
H_{v}=U_{1}(F_{v})=(G_{v})^{\mathrm{ab}}=(G^{\mathrm{ab}})_{v}
(note that
G_{v,\mathrm{der}}
is semisimple and simply-connected and asu such has trivial cohomology). We can therefore compute

(6.2.4)

\mathcal{T}_{\Delta}(f_{v})(h)=\int_{G_{v,\mathrm{der}}}f_{v}(hg)\,dg.

6.3.
General Places: Inequalities

We can also say a little at general places:

6.3.1.
Twisted Bernstein components

We recall some facts from
[
Rog88
, §6]
on Bernstein components for twisted groups. Let
G_{v}
be the
F_{v}
points of a connected reductive group and let
\widetilde{G}_{v}=G_{v}\rtimes\theta
be a twisted group for some automorphism
\theta
. We assume
G_{v}
has a minimal parabolic
P_{0}
and Levi factor
M_{0}
that are both
\theta
-stable. Let
\mathcal{L}(G_{v})
be the set of standard Levis of
G_{v}
with respect to
M_{0}
.

Definition 6.3.1
.

The twisted cuspidal supports for
\widetilde{G}_{v}
are pairs
(M,\sigma)
with
M\in\mathcal{L}(G_{v})
such that
(\theta M,\theta\sigma)
is conjugate to
(M,\sigma)
in
G_{v}
.

Definition 6.3.2
.

We say that a
\theta
-invariant irrep
\pi
of
G_{v}
has infinitesimal character
(M,\sigma)
if
\pi
is an irreducible subquotient of
\Ind_{MP_{0}}^{G_{v}}\sigma
.

Every
\theta
-invariant representation has an infinitesimal character that is a twisted cuspidal support.

Definition 6.3.3
.

Let
\tilde{\pi}
is an irrep of
\widetilde{G}_{v}
with non-zero (twisted trace) character. Then the infinitesimal character of
\tilde{\pi}
is that of
\tilde{\pi}|_{G_{v}}
.

Note that
\tilde{\pi}|_{G_{v}}
is necessarily
\theta
-invariant if
\tilde{\pi}
has non-zero character. Furthermore, all extensions of
\tilde{\pi}|_{G_{v}}
differ by a root of unity of order dividing that of
\theta
.

Definition 6.3.4
.

Let
(M,\sigma)
be a twisted cuspidal support. Its twisted Bernstein component is the set of irreps
\tilde{\pi}
with non-zero character on
\widetilde{G}_{v}
such that their infinitesimal characters are of the form
(M,\sigma\chi)
for
\chi
an unramified character of
M
(and
\sigma\chi

\theta
-invariant).

Part of the main result of
[
Rog88
]
is the following key point:

Lemma 6.3.5
.

Let
f_{v}
be a compactly supported, smooth function on
\widetilde{G}_{v}
. Then
\pi\mapsto\tr_{\pi}f_{v}
is supported on a finite number of twisted Bernstein components.

Now specialize to the case of
G=\tilde{G}(N)
:

Proposition 6.3.6
.

Let
\mathfrak{s}
be a twisted Bernstein component of
\widetilde{G}(N)_{v}
and let
H\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(N)
. Then there is a finite list
\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dotsc,\mathfrak{s}_{n}
of Bernstein components of
H_{v}
such that the Arthur packets
\Pi_{\psi_{v}}
for
\psi\in\Psi^{+}_{v}(H)
with
\widetilde{\pi}_{\psi}\in\mathfrak{s}
only contain representations in the
\mathfrak{s}_{i}
.

Proof.

This follows from three facts: first, the compatibility of twisted endoscopic transfer of characters with Jacquet modules as in diagram (C.4) in
[
Xu17
]
, second, the finiteness of
A
-packets, and third, the compatibility of transfer of characters with unramified character twist as in proposition 4.4 of
[
Oi21
]
.

We thank Masao Oi for pointing this out to us.
∎

6.3.2.
Inequalities

This lets us show:

Lemma 6.3.7
.

Let
v
be a finite place of
F
and
f_{v}
be a test function on
U_{E/F}(N)(F_{v})
. Let
dN_{1}=N
. Then there exists test function
\varphi_{v}
on
U_{E/F}(N_{1})(F_{v})
such that for all Arthur parameters
\psi_{v}=\psi_{1,v}[d]
with
\psi_{\star,v}
a parameter for
U_{E/F}(N_{\star})(F_{v})
and
\psi_{1,v}
cuspidal:

|\tr_{\psi_{v}}f_{v}|\leq(\tr_{\psi_{1,v}}\varphi_{v})^{d}.

Proof.

First,

\tr_{\psi_{v}}f_{v}=\tr_{\tilde{\pi}_{\psi_{v}}}f_{v}^{N}.

By Bernstein’s admissibility theorem (as used in
[
ST16
, Prop 9.6]
) there is
C
(without loss of generality,
C>1
) such that for all unirreps
\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}
of
\widetilde{G}(N)_{v}
,

|\tr_{\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}}f_{v}^{N}|\leq C.

Now consider the function from the unitary dual of
\widetilde{G}(N_{1})_{v}
to
\mathbb{C}
given by

\Phi_{0}:\tilde{\pi}\mapsto tr_{\tilde{\pi}[d]}f_{v}^{N}.

This is supported the on finitely many Bernstein components
\mathfrak{s}_{1},\dotsc,\mathfrak{s}_{i}
by Lemma
6.3.5
and since
the cuspidal support of
\tilde{\pi}[d]
determines that of
\tilde{\pi}
.

Therefore, the representations
\pi\in\Pi_{\psi_{1,v}}
for
\tilde{\pi}_{\pi_{1,v}}\in\mathfrak{s}_{i}
lie in a finite set of Bernstein components by Proposition
6.3.6
. Since
U_{E/F}(N_{1})
is not twisted, one can find a function
\varphi_{v}
on
U_{E/F}(N_{1})
so that
\tr_{\star}\varphi_{v}\geq C
on each of these components (e.g. a scalar multiple of the indicator function of a small enough maximal compact depending on the Bushnell-Kutzko types associated to the Bernstein components).

Finally, since
\psi_{1,v}
is cuspidal and therefore simple,
\tr_{\psi_{1,v}}\varphi_{v}
is a sum of various
\tr_{\pi}\varphi_{v}
and in particular larger. Therefore, this choice of
\varphi_{v}
suffices.
∎

Lemma 6.3.8
.

Let
v
be a place of
F
and
f_{v}
be a test function on
U_{E/F}(N)(F_{v})
. Let
N_{1}+\cdots+N_{n}=N
. Then there exists test functions
f_{i}
on
U_{E/F}(N_{i})(F_{v})
such that for all Arthur parameters
\psi_{v}=\psi_{1,v}\oplus\cdots\oplus\psi_{n,v}
with each
\psi_{\star,v}
a simple parameter for
U_{E/F}(N_{\star})(F_{v})
:

|\tr_{\psi_{v}}f_{v}|\leq\prod_{i}\tr_{\psi_{i,v}}f_{i,v}.

Proof.

This is the same argument as Lemma
6.3.7
.
∎

6.4.
General Places: Conjectural Equalities

At this time, exact equality results for transfers at general places are not known. This is the main reason for the restriction of our exact asymptotic result
11.4.1
to unitary groups for unramified
E/F
. We state the desired conjecture:

Conjecture 6.4.1

(Full Transfer)
.

Let
\Delta=((T_{i},d_{i},\eta_{i},\lambda_{i}))_{i}
be a shape and
\varphi_{v}
a trace-positive test function on
G_{v}
. Then there are trace-positive functions
\varphi_{i,v}
on the appropriate quasisplit unitary groups of rank
T_{i}
such that for all
\Delta\ni\psi=\bigoplus_{i}\psi_{i}[d_{i}]
with
\psi_{i}\in(T_{i},1,\eta_{i},\lambda_{i})
,

\tr_{\psi_{v}}(\varphi_{v})=\prod_{i}\tr_{\psi_{i,v}}(\varphi_{i,v}).

Except for the positivity statement, Conjecture
6.4.1
would be implied by two local conjectures on the
\widetilde{G}(N)_{v}
, the first of which is expected by experts:

Conjecture 6.4.2

(Stable Transfer)
.

Let
\Delta=(\Delta_{i})_{i}=(T_{i},d_{i},\eta_{i},\lambda_{i})_{i}
be a shape of rank
N
and
\varphi_{v}
a test function on
\widetilde{G}(N)_{v}
. Then there are test functions
\varphi_{i,v}
on each
\widetilde{G}(T_{i}d_{i})_{v}
such that for all choices of
\psi_{i}\in\Delta_{i}
,

\tr_{\widetilde{\pi}_{\psi_{v}}}(\varphi_{v})=\prod_{i}\tr_{\widetilde{\pi}_{\psi_{i,v}}}(\varphi_{i,v}).

Note that
\pi_{\psi}
is just the parabolic induction of the product of the
\pi_{\psi_{i}}
. In contrast, the relation between the between
\widetilde{\pi}_{\psi}
and the
\widetilde{\pi}_{\psi_{i}}
is more complicated and depends on the choice of extension in Section
2.2.2
.

The second necessary local conjecture is:

Conjecture 6.4.3

(Speh Transfer)
.

Let
\Delta=(T,d,\eta,\lambda)
be a simple shape and
\varphi_{v}
a test function on
\widetilde{G}(Td)
. Then there is a test function
\varphi^{\prime}_{v}
on
\widetilde{G}(T)
such that for all
\psi\in(T,1,\eta,\lambda)
,

\tr_{\widetilde{\pi}_{\psi_{v}[d]}}(\varphi_{v})=\tr_{\widetilde{\pi}_{\psi_{v}}}(\varphi^{\prime}_{v}).

For Speh transfer, even the relation between the untwisted representations
\pi_{\psi_{v}[d]}
and
\pi_{\psi_{v}}
is difficult due to the reducibility of the relevant parabolic inductions.

7.
Induction Step: General Shapes

In the following two sections, we prove the identities needed in the third, inductive step §
5.4
of the strategy outlined in §
5
. While our goal in §
5
was to produce exact formulas, in our applications we only need to find an asymptotic formula for
S^{H}_{\Delta}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty})
. Therefore, we will only need to solve two problems:

•

In this section, prove an upper bound (Theorem
7.3.3
) for a general shape to show that terms for non-dominant shapes are negligible.

•

In §
8
, find an exact asymptotic for terms with the types of shapes that could be dominant in our application.

This section heavily uses our notations from
1.6.2
for non-factorizable functions.

7.1.
Preliminary Bound

For any shape
\Delta
of rank
N
, recall from §
5.3
that

S^{N}_{\Delta}(f^{\infty}):=\sum_{\psi\in\Delta}S^{N}_{\psi}(f^{\infty})=\sum_{\psi\in\Delta}\epsilon^{H(\Delta)}_{\psi}(s^{H(\Delta)}_{\psi})m_{\psi}|S_{\psi}|^{-1}\tr_{\tilde{\pi}_{\psi}^{\infty}}(f^{\infty}).

Note that Proposition
5.3.1
still holds with subscripts of
\Delta
added to both sides.

By removing the
\epsilon
-sign, we also defined in
5.4
:

(7.1.1)

S^{|H(\Delta)|}_{\Delta}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}(f^{\infty})^{H(\Delta)})=S^{|N|}_{\Delta}(f^{\infty}):=\sum_{\psi\in\Delta}m_{\psi}|S_{\psi}|^{-1}\tr_{\widetilde{\pi}_{\psi}^{\infty}}(f^{\infty})

To approximate away
\epsilon_{\psi}
difficulties, we extend the main trick of
[
GG23
]
and prove a technical bound relating
S_{\Delta}^{N}
to
S_{\Delta^{\prime}}^{|H^{\prime}|}
terms for a suitable choice of
H^{\prime}
. It relies on a condition that we will assume henceforth for various test functions:

Definition 7.1.1
.

Let
S
be a (finite or infinite) set of places of
F
and
f_{S}
a function on
G_{S}
. We say that
f_{S}
is trace-positive on
S
if for all unirreps
\pi_{S}
of
G_{S}
,
\tr_{\pi_{S}}f_{S}\geq 0
.

Proposition 7.1.2
.

Let
H=H(\Delta)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(N)
. Assume that
f^{\infty}
is trace-positive. Then there is an elliptic endoscopic group
H^{\prime}=H_{1}\times H_{2}
of
H
such that for any infinitesimal character
\lambda^{\prime}
of
H^{\prime}
conjugate to
\lambda
over
H
:

|S^{N}_{\Delta}((f^{\infty})^{N})|\leq C\prodf_{i=1,2}S^{H_{i}}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda^{\prime}_{i}}(f^{\infty})^{i}).

Here we use shorthand: the data
\Delta,\lambda^{\prime}
, and
(f^{\infty})^{H^{\prime}}
all factor into components for
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
denoted with appropriate sub/superscripts. The
C
is a constant depending only on
H
and
\Delta
.

The
H^{\prime}
further satisfies that

S^{H_{1}}_{\Delta_{1}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda^{\prime}_{1}}(f^{\infty})^{1})S^{H_{2}}_{\Delta_{2}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda^{\prime}_{2}}(f^{\infty})^{2})=S^{|H_{1}|}_{\Delta_{1}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda^{\prime}_{1}}(f^{\infty})^{1})S^{|H_{2}|}_{\Delta_{2}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda^{\prime}_{2}}(f^{\infty})^{2}).

Proof.

Moving the absolute value within the sum from an intermediate computation in
5.3.1
, we know that

|S^{N}_{\Delta}((f^{\infty})^{N})|\leq\sum_{\psi\in\Delta}m_{\psi}|\mathcal{S}_{\psi}|^{-1}\left(\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^{H}_{\psi^{\infty}}}\tr_{\pi^{\infty}}(f^{\infty})\right).

Consider
\psi\in\Delta
. All such
\psi
have the same
s_{\psi}
and therefore the same
H^{\prime}
in the pair
(H^{\prime},\psi^{H^{\prime}})
corresponding to
s_{\psi}
as in Proposition
2.6.1
. The
\Out(\widehat{H}^{\prime})
-orbit of
(H^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})
that comes from
(\psi,s_{\psi})
is the subset with image in
\widehat{H}^{\prime}
of an
\widehat{H}
-conjugacy class. The elements of the orbit can therefore be determined by their infinitesimal character at
\infty
—i.e. the choice of
\lambda^{\prime}
uniformly determines a unique choice of parameter
\psi^{\prime}
on
H^{\prime}
for each
\psi\in\Delta
.

Denote by
f^{\prime}
the transfer to
H^{\prime}
. By Theorem
2.6.5
applied to
H(s_{\psi})=H^{\prime}
, iterations of the computations of Proposition
5.3.1
, and the twisted character identity away from infinity:

S_{\psi^{\prime}}^{H^{\prime}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda^{\prime}}(f^{\infty})^{\prime})=\epsilon^{G}_{\psi}(s_{\psi}s_{\psi})m_{\psi^{\prime}}|\mathcal{S}_{\psi^{\prime}}|^{-1}\left(\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^{H}_{\psi^{\infty}}}\eta^{\psi^{\infty}}_{\pi^{\infty}}(s_{\psi}s_{\psi})\tr_{\pi^{\infty}}(f^{\infty})\right)\\
=m_{\psi^{\prime}}|\mathcal{S}_{\psi^{\prime}}|^{-1}\left(\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^{H}_{\psi^{\infty}}}\tr_{\pi^{\infty}}(f^{\infty})\right),

with the second equality following from
s_{\psi}^{2}=1
. The stable multiplicity formula
2.6.3
also gives

S_{\psi^{\prime}}^{H^{\prime}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda^{\prime}}(f^{\infty})^{\prime})=S_{\psi^{\prime}}^{|H^{\prime}|}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda^{\prime}}(f^{\infty})^{\prime})

since
\epsilon^{H^{\prime}}_{\psi^{\prime}}(s_{\psi^{\prime}})
. Summing over
\psi\in\Delta
on one side and the corresponding
\psi^{\prime}
on the other gives

|S^{N}_{\Delta}((f^{\infty})^{N})|\leq\frac{m_{\psi}|\mathcal{S}_{\psi}|^{-1}}{m_{\psi^{\prime}}|\mathcal{S}_{\psi^{\prime}}|^{-1}}S_{\Delta}^{H^{\prime}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda^{\prime}}(f^{\infty})^{\prime})

where
S^{H^{\prime}}_{\Delta}
is defined by summing over parameters that pushforward to something of the right shape on
H
. Both desired expressions then follow from factoring the
H^{\prime}
term into terms for
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
in these last two formulas.
∎

7.2.
Reduction to Simple Shapes

We now bound
S_{\Delta}
in terms
S_{\Delta^{\prime}}
for cuspidal
\Delta^{\prime}
. We do this in two steps: in this section we reduce to simple shapes and in the next we pass from simple to cuspidal.

To apply results of §
6
, we present test functions in a particular form: choose
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(N)
and let
S=\infty\sqcup S_{s}\sqcup S_{b}
be finite sets of places such that
S_{s}
is split and
S_{b}
contains all the “bad” ramified places. Write test functions as:

f^{\infty}=\varphi_{S_{b}}f_{S_{s}}f^{S}

where
\varphi_{S_{b}}
and
f_{S_{s}}
are arbitrary and
f^{S}\in\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{ur}}(G^{S})
.

Proposition 7.2.1
.

Consider the shape
\Delta=(T_{i},d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})_{i}
. Assume that
f_{S_{s}}
and
f^{S}
are trace-positive. Then

S^{|N|}_{\Delta}((f_{S_{s}}f_{S_{b}}f^{S})^{N})\leq C_{\Delta}\prodf_{i}S^{T_{i}d_{i}}_{(T_{i},d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})}(((f_{S_{s}})_{M,i}\varphi_{i,S_{b}}\mathcal{T}_{i}f^{S})^{T_{i}d_{i}})

for trace-positive
\varphi_{i,S_{b}}
and a constant
C_{\Delta}
that only depends on
\Delta
.

Proof.

We first work with a single
\psi=\tau_{1}[d_{1}]\oplus\cdots\oplus\tau_{k}[d_{k}]\in\Delta
and compute the terms in the stable multiplicity formula. Iteratively applying Lemma
6.1.2
on transfer for split groups, we realize

M=\prod_{i}\mathrm{GL}_{T_{i}d_{i}}(F_{S_{s}})

as a Levi of
G(F_{S_{s}})\simeq\mathrm{GL}_{N}(F_{S_{s}})
with

\tr_{\psi_{S_{s}}}f_{S_{s}}=\prodf_{i}\tr_{\tau_{i,S_{s}}[d_{i}]}(f_{S_{s}})_{M,i},

where
(\star)_{M,i}
is the
i
th factor of the constant term map to
M
.

For the places not in
S
, Lemma
6.2.2
gives that

\tr_{\psi^{S}}f^{S}=\prodf_{i}\tr_{\tau_{i}^{S}[d_{i}]}\mathcal{T}_{i}f^{S},

and multiplying over places in
S_{b}
, Lemma
6.3.8
constructs
\varphi_{i,S_{b}}
so that

|\tr_{\psi_{S_{b}}}(\varphi_{S_{b}})|\leq\prod_{i}\tr_{\tau_{i,S_{b}}[d_{i}]}(\varphi_{i,S_{b}}).

Next, each
\epsilon_{\tau_{i}[d_{i}]}
is trivial for each of the
\tau_{i}[d_{i}]
since they are simple. Multiplying together the above trace identities, we have

(7.2.1)

\left|S^{|N|}_{\psi}((f_{S_{s}}\varphi_{S_{b}}f^{S})^{N})\right|\leq C_{\Delta}\left|\prodf_{i}S^{T_{i}d_{i}}_{\tau_{i}[d_{i}]}(((f_{S_{s}})_{M,i}\varphi_{i,S_{b}}\mathcal{T}_{i}f^{S})^{T_{i}d_{i}})\right|

for some constant
C_{\Delta}
that only depends on
\Delta
.

By regularity of the infinitesimal character, none of the
(T_{i},d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})
appear with multiplicity more than
1
. Therefore, summing (
7.2.1
) over all
\psi\in\Delta
is the same as iteratively summing over each factor
\tau_{i}[d_{i}]\in(T_{i},d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})
.

To remove the absolute values on the right side of the summed equation,
(f_{S_{s}})_{M,i}
and
\mathcal{T}_{i}f^{S}
satisfy the same positivity condition: positivity for
(f_{S_{s}})_{M,i}
follows by “adjointness” of constant term and parabolic induction. That for
\mathcal{T}_{i}f^{S}
comes from the “adjointness” between
\mathcal{T}_{i}
and pushforward of Satake parameter. The
\varphi_{i,S_{b}}
satisfy positivity by construction. Together with the fact that the
\epsilon_{\tau_{i}[d_{i}]}
are trivial, this guarantees that the absolute value can be removed on the right of (
7.2.1
).
∎

Doing the bookkeeping for what exactly
\Delta_{1},\Delta_{2}
are in Proposition
7.1.2
lets us bound
S^{N}
instead of
S^{|N|}
:

Corollary 7.2.2
.

Let the notation be as in the above discussion and assume that
f_{S_{s}}
,
\varphi_{S_{b}}
, and
f^{S}
are all trace-positive.
Then for some functions
\varphi^{\prime}_{i,S_{b}}
and some constant
C_{\Delta}
only depending on
\Delta
,

|S^{N}_{\Delta}((f_{S_{s}}f_{S_{b}}f^{S})^{N})|\leq C_{\Delta}\prodf_{i}S^{T_{i}d_{i}}_{(T_{i},d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})}(((f_{S_{s}})_{M,i}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,S_{b}}\mathcal{T}_{i}f^{S})^{T_{i}d_{i}}).

Furthermore,
(f_{S_{s}})_{M,i}
,
\varphi^{\prime}_{i,S_{b}}
and
\mathcal{T}_{i}f^{S}
satisfy the same positivity condition.

Proof.

First, apply Proposition
7.1.2
to get a bound by
S^{|N|}
terms and then apply Proposition
7.2.1
to the two terms on the right side of the bound in
7.1.2
. Note that we need to use the full fundamental lemma (see e.g. Theorem 5.4.2 in
[
Dal22
]
) to choose a transfer
f^{S}
to
H^{\prime}
that is dual to the pushforward of Satake parameters. The
\varphi^{\prime}
are constructed through the above discussion applied to transfer to
H^{\prime}
of
f_{S_{b}}
. Finally, the transfer of
f_{S_{s}}
to
H^{\prime}
is given by taking constant terms since we are in a degenerate case of
H^{\prime}_{S_{s}}
being a Levi subgroup.

The positivity condition for the various transfers follows from the exact same arguments as in the last proof. ∎

7.3.
Full Bound

Now we reduce from simple to cuspidal shapes. Let
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(N)
and
\Delta=(T,d,\lambda,\eta)
a simple shape for
G
, so that all
\psi\in\Delta
are of the form
\psi=\tau[d]
. As before, choose a test function

(7.3.1)

f^{\infty}=f_{S_{s}}f_{S_{b}}f^{S}

on
G
where all places in
S_{s}
are split,
S_{b}
contains all ramified places, and
f^{S}\in\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{ur}}(G^{S})
. Further assume:

•

f_{S_{s}}f_{S_{b}}
is supported on the kernel of
|\det|_{S_{s}\sqcup S_{b}}
,

•

f_{S_{s}}
and
f^{S}
are trace-positive.

The argument is very similar to the previous section. First,

|S_{\Delta}^{M}((f_{S_{s}}f_{S_{b}}f^{S})^{M})|\leq\sum_{\psi\in\Delta}|S^{M}_{\psi}((f_{S_{s}}f_{S_{b}}f^{S})^{M})|\\
=\sum_{\psi\in\Delta}m_{\psi}|\mathcal{S}_{\psi}|(\tr_{\psi_{S_{s}}}f_{S_{s}})(\tr_{\psi^{S}}f^{S})|\tr_{\psi_{S_{b}}}f_{S_{b}}|.

For all
\psi\in\Delta
, apply Lemma
6.1.3
to get a Levi
M_{S_{s}}\simeq{\mathrm{GL}_{T}}^{d}
of
\mathrm{GL}_{M}(F_{S_{s}})
so that

\tr_{\psi_{S_{s}}}f_{S_{s}}\leq(\tr_{\tau_{S_{s}}}(f_{S_{s}})_{M,1})^{d\oplus}.

We also apply
6.3.7
to construct the functions
\varphi^{\prime}_{S_{b}}
satisfying

|\tr_{\psi_{S_{b}}}\varphi_{S_{b}}|\leq(\tr_{\tau_{S_{b}}}\varphi^{\prime}_{S_{b}})^{d\oplus}

since the
\tau_{S_{b}}
are necessarily cuspidal. Note that the
\varphi^{\prime}_{S_{b}}
so constructed is trace-positive by definition. Applying Lemma
6.2.2
then gives

(7.3.2)

|\tr_{\psi^{\infty}}(f_{S_{s}}\varphi_{S_{b}}f^{S})|\leq(\tr_{\tau^{\infty}}((f_{S_{s}})_{M,1}\varphi^{\prime}_{S_{b}}\mathcal{T}f^{S}))^{d\oplus}.

Finally, summing over all
\tau[d]\in\Delta
gives that:

Proposition 7.3.1
.

With notation and conditions from the above discussion:

|S_{\Delta}^{M}((f_{S_{s}}\varphi_{S_{b}}f^{S})^{M})|\leq C_{\Delta}(S_{\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta}}^{T}((f_{S_{s}})_{M,1}\varphi^{\prime}_{S_{b}}\mathcal{T}f^{S})^{T})^{d\oplus}

for some constant
C
that only depends on
\Delta
. Recall that
(f_{S_{s}})_{M,1}
and
\mathcal{T}f^{S}
are trace positive and we can choose
\varphi^{\prime}_{S_{b}}
to be so too.

Proof.

Expanding out and using that
\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta}
is simple:

(S_{\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta}}^{T}((f_{S_{s}})_{M,1}\varphi^{\prime}_{S_{b}}\mathcal{T}f^{S})^{T})^{d\oplus}\\
=\sum_{\tau\in\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta}}m_{\psi}^{d}|\mathcal{S}_{\psi}|^{d}(\tr_{\tau^{\infty}}((f_{S_{s}})_{M,1}\varphi^{\prime}_{S_{b}}\mathcal{T}f^{S}))^{d\oplus}+\text{cross terms},

where trace-positivity comes from the conditions in the above discussion and arguments similar to Corollary
7.2.2
. Since
m_{\psi}
and
|S_{\psi}|
only depend on
\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta}
, the inequality then follows from (
7.3.2
) since trace positivity gives that the cross terms are all positive. We use here that
\Delta
is simple so
\epsilon_{\psi}(s_{\psi})
is identically 1. ∎

We will need a slight technical variation of this:

Proposition 7.3.2
.

Fix notation and conditions as in Proposition
7.3.1
. Assume there is a constant
B
such that for all
\tau\in\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta}
:

|\tr_{\tilde{\tau}^{\infty}}(((f_{S_{s}})_{M,1}\varphi^{\prime}_{S_{b}}\mathcal{T}f^{S})^{T})|^{\oplus}\leq B

where the
\oplus
represents a sum over factorizable summands. Then

|S_{\Delta}^{M}((f_{S_{s}}\varphi_{S_{b}}f^{S})^{M})|\leq CB^{d-1}S_{\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta}}^{T}(((f_{S_{s}})_{M,1}\varphi^{\prime}_{S_{b}}\mathcal{T}f^{S})^{T})^{\oplus}

for some constant
C
that only depends on
\Delta
.

Proof.

This is the same argument as
7.3.1
except we bound

\sum_{\tau\in\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta}}m_{\psi}^{d}|\mathcal{S}_{\psi}|^{d}(\tr_{\tau^{\infty}}((f_{S_{s}})_{M,1}\varphi^{\prime}_{S_{b}}\mathcal{T}f^{S}))^{d\oplus}

directly instead of adding in cross terms.
∎

Our final bound for a general shape then becomes:

Proposition 7.3.3
.

Let
\Delta=(T_{i},d_{i},\eta_{i},\lambda_{i})_{i}
and
G=H(\Delta)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(N)
. Let

f^{\infty}=f_{S_{s}}\varphi_{s_{b}}f^{S}

be a test function on
G^{\infty}
where
S_{s}
is all split places,
S_{b}
contains all the ramified places, and
f^{S}\in\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{ur}}(G^{S})
. We further require:

•

f_{S_{s}}
is supported on the kernel of
|\det|_{S_{s}}
,

•

f_{S_{s}}
,
\varphi_{S_{b}}
, and
f^{S}
are trace-positive.

Let
f^{\infty}=f_{S_{s}}\varphi_{s_{b}}f^{S}
be a test function as in (
7.3.1
). Then there is a Levi subgroup

M\simeq\prod_{i}\mathrm{GL}_{T_{i}}(F_{v})^{d_{i}}

of
G(F_{S_{s}})\simeq\mathrm{GL}_{N}(F_{S_{s}})
and functions
\varphi^{\prime}_{i,S_{b}}
such that

|S_{\Delta}^{N}((f_{S_{s}}f_{S_{b}}f^{S})^{N})|\leq C_{\Delta}\prodf_{i}(S^{T_{i}}_{\Sigma_{\lambda_{i},\eta_{i}}}(((f_{S_{s}})_{M,i}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,S_{b}}\mathcal{T}_{i}f^{S})^{T_{i}}))^{d_{i}\oplus}.

Here
(f_{S_{s}})_{M,i}
is the restriction of the constant term to
M
to the first factor in
\mathrm{GL}_{T_{i}}(F_{v})^{d_{i}}
,
\mathcal{T}_{i}
is defined similarly, and the constant
C_{\Delta}
only depends on
\Delta
.

Finally, we may choose
\varphi^{\prime}_{i,S_{b}}
so that
(f_{S_{s}})_{M,i}
,
\varphi^{\prime}_{i,S_{b}}
and
\mathcal{T}_{i}f^{S}
satisfy the same support and positivity conditions
•
‣
7.3
as
f_{S_{s}},f_{S_{b}}
, and
f^{S}
.

Proof.

The inequality comes from applying
7.2.2
and then
7.3.1
. The first thing to check is that the necessary conditions for
7.3.1
still hold after applying
7.2.2
. Positivity is guaranteed by the second implication of
7.2.2
. Support follows from the integral formula for constant term.

The final
\varphi^{\prime}_{i,S_{b}}
,
(f_{S_{s}})_{M,i}
, and
\mathcal{T}_{i}f^{S}
satisfy the bulleted conditions by the last implications of
7.3.1
.
∎

8.
Induction Step: Particular Shapes

We compute more detailed information for certain specific kinds of shapes with the goal of providing exact asymptotics:

8.1.
Shapes of Characters

Let
\Delta=(1,d,\lambda,\eta)
be a shape for
H=H(\Delta)
. If
\psi\in\Delta
, then the packet
\Pi_{\psi_{\infty}}
constructed by Adams-Johnson, see §
3.2.2
, is a singleton containing a character
\xi_{\infty}
of
H
. Let
\mathrm{EP}_{\xi_{\infty}}=\vol(A_{G,\infty}\backslash G_{\infty}/G_{\mathrm{der},\infty})^{-1}\xi^{-1}_{\infty}
be the corresponding Euler-Poincaré function (note that by our assumptions on
E/F
,
H
is cuspidal in the sense of
[
Art89a
]
so
\xi^{-1}_{\infty}
is a valid test function factor for the global trace formula).

Proposition 8.1.1
.

Let
\Delta=(1,d,\lambda,\eta)
a shape for
H
and
\xi_{\infty}
be as above. Then,

S^{H}_{\Delta}(\mathrm{EP}_{\xi_{\infty}}f^{\infty})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\chi\in\mathcal{AC}_{\mathrm{disc}}(H)\\
\chi_{\infty}=\xi_{\infty}\end{subarray}}\tr_{\chi^{\infty}}f^{\infty},

where
\mathcal{AC}_{\mathrm{disc}}(H)
is the set of one-dimensional representations in the discrete automorphic spectrum of
H
.

Proof.

If
\psi\in\Delta
, any
\pi\in\Pi_{\psi}
has
\pi_{\infty}=\xi_{\infty}
as explained above. This implies that
\pi
is one-dimensional by a well-known result (see
[
KST16
, Lem 6.2]
for example). Furthermore,
\psi
is simple, so as distributions,

S_{\psi}=I_{\psi}=\sum_{\pi\in\Pi_{\psi}}\tr_{\pi}.

Evaluating at our test function,

S_{\psi}(\mathrm{EP}_{\xi_{\infty}}f^{\infty})=I_{\psi}(\mathrm{EP}_{\xi_{\infty}}f^{\infty})=\sum_{\pi\in\Pi_{\psi}}\tr_{\pi^{\infty}}(f^{\infty}).

On the other hand, any
\chi\in\mathcal{AC}_{\mathrm{disc}}(H)
appears in some
A
-packet
\Pi_{\psi}
. If
\chi_{\infty}=\xi_{\infty}
, then since
H
is quasisplit,
\psi_{\infty}
has full Arthur-
\mathrm{SL}_{2}
and infinitesimal character
\lambda
, which forces
\chi\in\Delta
. Furthermore, we recall that characters appear with multiplicity at most one in the automorphic spectrum (realized as functions by evaluation). Therefore every such
\chi
can only appear in one packet. In total, the union over
\Pi_{\psi}
for
\psi\in\Delta
is exactly the subset of
\mathcal{AC}_{\mathrm{disc}}(H)
with infinite component
\xi_{\infty}
and this union is disjoint.

Summing over
\psi\in\Delta
then finishes the argument.
∎

Next,
\mathcal{AC}_{\mathrm{disc}}(H)
are the characters of

\Xi(H):=H(F)^{\mathrm{ab}}\backslash H(\mathbb{A})^{\mathrm{ab}}

so we can therefore write

S^{H}_{\Delta}(\mathrm{EP}_{\xi_{\infty}}f^{\infty})=\frac{1}{\vol(H^{\mathrm{ab}}_{\infty})}\sum_{\chi\in\Xi(H)^{\vee}}\widehat{\xi_{\infty}^{-1}f^{\infty,\mathrm{ab}}}(\chi),

where
f^{\infty,\mathrm{ab}}
is the pushforward (by integration against
H(\mathbb{A}^{\infty})_{\mathrm{der}}
) of
f^{\infty}
to
H(\mathbb{A}^{\infty})^{\mathrm{ab}}
. Then:

Corollary 8.1.2
.

Let
H=H(\Delta)
for
\Delta=(1,d,\lambda,\eta)
corresponding to a character
\xi_{\infty}
on
H_{\infty}
. Then, with
f^{\infty,\mathrm{ab}}
as above:

S^{H}_{\Delta}(\mathrm{EP}_{\xi_{\infty}}f^{\infty})=\frac{\vol(H(F)^{\mathrm{ab}}\backslash H(\mathbb{A})^{\mathrm{ab}})}{\vol(H^{\mathrm{ab}}_{\infty})}\sum_{\gamma\in H(F)^{\mathrm{ab}}}\xi_{\infty}^{-1}f^{\infty,\mathrm{ab}}(\gamma).

Proof.

Since
H^{\mathrm{ab}}(F)\backslash H^{\mathrm{ab}}(\mathbb{A})
is compact for our specific
H
, we get that
H(F)^{\mathrm{ab}}
is cocompact in
H(\mathbb{A})^{\mathrm{ab}}
. Since
H(F)^{\mathrm{ab}}
is a subgroup of
H^{\mathrm{ab}}(F)
and
H(\mathbb{A})^{\mathrm{ab}}
is an open subgroup of
H^{\mathrm{ab}}(\mathbb{A})
, discreteness of
H^{\mathrm{ab}}(F)
in
H^{\mathrm{ab}}(\mathbb{A})
gives discreteness of
H(F)^{\mathrm{ab}}
in
H(\mathbb{A})^{\mathrm{ab}}
.

Therefore Poisson summation gives the result.
∎

Our
H(\Delta)
is necessarily isomorphic as a reductive group to
U(N)
so we will have
H^{\mathrm{ab}}=U(1)
in the above.

8.2.
Odd GSK Shapes

We will eventually focus on shapes similar to the Saito-Kurokawa case (d) at the end of
[
Art04
]
:

Definition 8.2.1
.

We say a shape
\Delta=(T_{i},d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})_{1\leq i\leq k}
is
generalized Saito-Kurokawa
or
GSK
if:

•

d_{1}=1
and for all
i>1
,
T_{i}=1
,

•

The
d_{i}
are distinct integers.

We furthermore say it is
odd GSK
if:

•

The
d_{i}
are all odd.

Note
.

We define GSK to isolate the shapes that will contribute as main terms in our final application to counting representations in §
11
. Our techniques apply slightly more generally—in particular, the condition that the
d_{i}
are distinct only appears because Lemma
11.2.2
forces any dominant term to satisfy it.

We also leave out a case of “equiparity GSK”, where
all

T_{i}=1
and all
d_{i}
are even. This is for uniformity of later theorem statements.

We will get exact asymptotic bounds for such
S_{\Delta}
since these are the ones where the only Speh representations that appear are characters. Understanding more general shapes would require the Speh Transfer Conjecture
6.4.3
.

We recall the setup of Sections
7.2
and
7.3
and follow a similar argument: choose
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(N)
and pick finite sets of places
S=\infty\sqcup S_{s}\sqcup S_{b}
where
S_{s}
is all split and
S_{b}
contains all the ramified places. We will look at test functions of the form:

f^{\infty}=\varphi_{S_{b}}f_{S_{s}}f^{S}

with
\varphi_{S_{b}}
and
f_{S_{s}}
arbitrary and
f^{S}\in\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{ur}}(G^{S})
. We will further assume that the pair
(\Delta,\varphi_{S_{b}})
satisfies the Stable Transfer Conjecture
6.4.1
and that
\Delta
is odd GSK.

Consider

\psi=\tau_{1}[d_{1}]\oplus\cdots\oplus\tau_{k}[d_{k}]\in\Delta
.
For all such
\psi
,
Lemma
6.1.2
gives Levi

M=\prod_{i}\mathrm{GL}_{T_{i}d_{i}}(F_{S_{s}})

of
G(F_{S_{s}})\simeq\mathrm{GL}_{N}(F_{S_{s}})
so that

\tr_{\psi_{S_{s}}}f_{S_{s}}=\prodf_{i}\tr_{\tau_{i,S_{s}}[d_{i}]}(f_{S_{s}})_{M,i},

and Lemma
6.2.2
gives that

\tr_{\psi^{S}}f^{S}=\prodf_{i}\tr_{\tau_{i}^{S}[d_{i}]}(\mathcal{T}_{i}f^{S}).

Note that the constant terms and
\mathcal{T}_{i}f^{S}
satisfy the positivity condition by various “adjointesses” of trace and various transfers.

Finally, our assumption that
\varphi_{S_{b}}
satisfies Conjecture
6.4.1
gives
\varphi_{i,S_{b}}
such that

\tr_{\psi_{S_{b}}}\varphi_{S_{b}}=\prodf_{i}\tr_{\tau_{i,S_{s}}[d_{i}]}(\varphi_{i,S_{b}})

where the
\varphi_{i,S_{b}}
are trace-positive.

This produces:

Proposition 8.2.2
.

With notation and conditions as above (in particular, that the pair
(\Delta,\varphi_{S_{b}})
satisfies Conjecture
6.4.1
and
\Delta
is odd GSK),

S^{H}_{\Delta}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}\varphi_{S_{b}}f_{S_{s}}f^{S})=2^{-k+1}\prodf_{i}S^{H_{i}}_{(T_{i},d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda_{i}[d_{i}]}(f_{S_{s}})_{M,i}\varphi_{i,S_{b}}\mathcal{T}_{i}f^{S}),

where
H_{i}=H(T_{i},d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})
.

Proof.

As in Section
7.2
, we multiply together and sum the trace equalities above. Note that
\Delta
having all
d_{i}
of the same parity gives that
S^{H}_{\Delta}=S^{|H|}_{\Delta}
by Lemma
2.4.3
. Furthermore, since we are working with unitary groups,
m_{\psi}=1
always. Finally, for
\psi\in\Delta
as above, since the
\tau_{i}[d_{i}]
are simple,

|\mathcal{S}_{\psi}|^{-1}\prod_{i}|\mathcal{S}_{\tau_{i}[d_{i}]}|=2^{-k+1}.

This computes all the terms in the stable multiplicty formula.
∎

9.
Level-Aspect Asymptotics

9.1.
Setup

In this section, we use the strategy outlined in §
5
to compute asymptotics of
S^{G}_{\Delta}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty})
for a specific sequence of
f^{\infty}
.

Fix
N
and
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{sim}}(N)
such that
G=H(\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta})
. We use the setup from
[
ST16
]
. First, as some notation:

•

K:=K^{G}_{R}
is a choice of hyperspecial subgroup of
G
at a set of unramified places
R
. Similarly define
K^{G,R}
if
R
contains all ramified and infinite places.

•

\dim\lambda
for
\lambda
a regular, integral infinitesimal character is the dimension of the associated finite-dimensional representation on
\mathrm{GL}_{n}\mathbb{C}
.

•

K(\mathfrak{n}):=K^{G}_{R}(\mathfrak{n})
for
\mathfrak{n}
an ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{F}
supported on a set of unramified places
R
is the principal congruence subgroup of level
\mathfrak{n}
:

K^{G}_{R}(\mathfrak{n})=\prod_{v|R}K^{G}_{v}(q_{v}^{v(\mathfrak{n})}),

where
K^{G}_{v}(q_{v}^{v(\mathfrak{n})})
is the
\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
-points the integral model defined by hyperspecial
K^{G}_{v}
congruent to
1\pmod{\mathfrak{q}_{v}^{v(\mathfrak{n})}}
. Define
K^{G,R}(\mathfrak{n})
similarly away from a set of places
R
containing all ramified and infinite places.

•

G_{\infty}^{c}
is the compact form of
G_{\infty}
. A choice of measure on
G_{\infty}
induces one on the compact form
G_{\infty}^{c}
in the standard way through top forms on
G_{\infty,\mathbb{C}}
.

Then, fix:

•

A finite set of finite places
S_{0}
including all those where
E/F
is ramified,

•

An arbitrary test function
\varphi_{S_{0}}
at
S_{0}
,

•

A finite set of finite places
S_{1}
disjoint from
S_{0}
,

•

An
f_{S_{1}}\in\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{ur}}(G_{S_{1}})^{\leq\kappa}
for some
\kappa
,

•

An ideal
\mathfrak{n}
of
F
relatively prime to
2,3,S_{0},S_{1}
and that is all split in
E
. We will let
\mathfrak{n}
vary and consider asymptotics as
|\mathfrak{n}|\to\infty
.

•

S:=\infty\cup S_{0}\cup S_{1}
.

Then define the test function:

f^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}=\varphi_{S_{0}}f_{S_{1}}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K^{G,S}(\mathfrak{n})}

where
\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K^{G,S}(\mathfrak{n})}
is the indicator function normalized
by volume
to have integral
1
.

We will also need some constants related to
\mathfrak{n}
:

•

The norm

|\mathfrak{n}|:=\prod_{v|\mathfrak{n}}q_{v}^{v(\mathfrak{n})}.

•

Euler factors: for
n,n_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{n}(\mathfrak{n}):=\prod_{v|\mathfrak{n}}(1-q_{v}^{-1})(1-q_{v}^{-2})\cdots(1-q_{v}^{-n}),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{-n}(\mathfrak{n}):=\prod_{v|\mathfrak{n}}(1+q_{v}^{-1})(1+q_{v}^{-2})\cdots(1+q_{v}^{-n}),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\pm n_{1},\dotsc,\pm n_{k}}(\mathfrak{n}):=\Gamma_{\pm n_{1}}(\mathfrak{n})\cdots\Gamma_{\pm n_{k}}(\mathfrak{n}).

Our initial input will be the level-aspect bounds of
[
ST16
]
.

Theorem 9.1.1

(Special case of
[
ST16
, Thm 9.16]
)
.

With notation defined as above, there are constants
A,B,C,D,E
depending only on
G
with
C\geq 1
such that whenever
|\mathfrak{n}|\geq Dq_{S_{1}}^{E\kappa}
,

|\mathfrak{n}|^{-\dim G}\Gamma_{N}(\mathfrak{n})^{-1}I^{G}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})=\Lambda+O(|\mathfrak{n}|^{-C}q_{S_{1}}^{A+B\kappa}),

where we define the mass:

\Lambda=\Lambda(G,f,\varphi)=\varphi_{S_{0}}(1)f_{S_{1}}(1)\frac{\dim\lambda}{|\Pi_{\mathrm{disc}}(\lambda)|}\frac{\vol(G(F)\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{F}))}{\vol(K^{S})\vol(G_{\infty}^{c})}.

Proof.

Since only split primes divide
\mathfrak{n}
, we have

[K:K(\mathfrak{n})]=|\mathfrak{n}|^{\dim G}\Gamma_{N}(\mathfrak{n})

by a standard formula for the sizes of the
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{v}/q_{v}^{v(\mathfrak{n})}\mathcal{O}_{v})
.
∎

When
S_{0}=S_{1}=\emptyset
, the quotient of volumes is the modified Tamagawa number computed in
[
ST16
, Cor 6.14]
:

(9.1.1)

\frac{\vol(G(F)\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{F}))}{\vol(K^{\infty})\vol(G_{\infty}^{c})}=\tau^{\prime}(G)=2^{-(N-1)\deg F}\tau(G)L(\mathrm{Mot}_{G})|\Omega_{G}||\Omega_{G_{\infty}}^{c}|^{-1}

where
\tau(G)
is the Tamagawa number,
L(\mathrm{Mot}_{G})
is the
L
-value of the motive from
[
Gro97
]
, and
\Omega_{G_{\infty}}^{c}
is the Weyl group of the maximal compact at
\infty
.

Since we are not tracking explicit values for
A,B,C,D,E
, we will allow them to change throughout the following argument.

9.2.
Bounds on Stable Trace

To extend
9.1.1
to
S^{G}
, we next recall a standard formula:

Lemma 9.2.1
.

Let
G
be an unramified reductive group over
F
,
\mathfrak{n}_{i}
an ideal relatively prime to all places where
G
is ramified, and
M
a Levi component of parabolic subgroup
P
. Then we have identity of indicator functions normalized by volume:

(\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K^{G}(\mathfrak{n})})_{M}=I(\mathfrak{n})\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K^{M}(\mathfrak{n})},

where we recall that
(\star)_{M}
denotes the constant term and

I(\mathfrak{n})=[K:K\cap K(\mathfrak{n})P].

Furthermore, if
G=\mathrm{GL}_{N}
then

I(\mathfrak{n}_{i})=(1+O(|\mathfrak{n}|^{-2}))|\mathfrak{n}|^{\dim G/P}\Gamma_{-1}(\mathfrak{n})^{\sigma(M)},

where

\sigma(M)=\rank_{\mathrm{ss}}G-\rank_{\mathrm{ss}}M.

Proof.

This is well known—see for example the proof of Lemma 5.2 in
[
MS19
]
.
∎

Note for intuition later that
\dim G/P=1/2(\dim G-\dim M)
. Using this:

Proposition 9.2.2
.

Let
f_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\infty}
and
\Lambda
be as in Section
9.1
. Then there are constants
A,B,C,D,E
depending only on
G
with
C\geq 1
such that whenever
|\mathfrak{n}|\geq Dq_{S_{1}}^{E\kappa}
,

|\mathfrak{n}|^{-\dim G}\Gamma_{N}(\mathfrak{n})^{-1}S^{G}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})=\Lambda+O(|\mathfrak{n}|^{-C}q_{S_{1}}^{A+B\kappa}).

Proof.

We apply Theorem
5.2.1
and apply Theorem
9.1.1
to each term. This
is a much simpler version of the main result of
[
Dal22
]
so we present it tersely.

We need only show that all the non-
I^{G}
summands can be put into the error term:

•

In this case of unitary groups, there are a finite number of such terms.

•

We can ignore dependence on
\varphi_{S_{0}}
.

•

Lemma 5.5.4 in
[
Dal22
]
allows us to bound the value at
1
and the support of the
f_{S_{1}}^{\mathcal{H}}
.

•

Lemma
6.1.2
lets us iteratively use Lemma
9.2.1
to bound the
(\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K^{G,S}(\mathfrak{n}_{i})})^{\mathcal{H}}
.

•

Averaging Corollary 5.1.6 in
[
Dal22
]
shows that the transfer of the EP-function is a linear combination of a number of EP-functions uniformly bounded over
\mathcal{H}
.

Putting all this together,
9.1.1
shows that the sum of the non-
I^{G}
terms is bounded above by the claimed error as long as we extremize
A,B,C,D,E
appropriately over all hyperendoscopic groups.
∎

9.3.
The Induction

Now we induct to bound the limit multiplicities restricted to specific shapes. Their are two pieces to this argument—first, as a consequence of Proposition
5.3.1
:

(9.3.1)

S^{G}_{\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})=S^{G}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}H(\Delta)=H(\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta})\\
\text{inf. char}(\Delta)=\lambda\\
\Delta\neq\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta}\end{subarray}}S^{G}_{\Delta}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}}).

Second, the bound in Proposition
7.3.3
lets us show that the non-
\Sigma
terms
S^{G}_{\Delta}
have limit multiplicities controlled by the groups
M
from which they are lifts.

We start with a technical trick that gives us the trace-positivity conditions needed to apply the results of the previous section:

Lemma 9.3.1
.

Let
f_{v}
be a test function on
G_{v}
. Then there are trace-positive functions
f_{1},\dotsc,f_{k}
such that

f_{v}=\lambda_{1}f_{1}+\cdots+\lambda_{k}f_{k}.

Furthermore, if
f_{v}\in\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{ur}}(G_{v})^{\leq\kappa}
, then so are the
f_{i}
and

\sum_{i}|\lambda_{i}|\leq C,\qquad\sum_{i}\|f_{i}\|_{\infty}\leq C\|f_{v}\|_{\infty}

for some uniform constant
C
.

Proof.

The first statement holds in great generality by the Dixmier-Malliavin decomposition theorem as used
[
Sau97
, Lem 3.5]
. We recall some details:

We can without loss of generality assume
f_{v}^{*}=f_{v}
by symmetrizing:

f_{v}=\left(\frac{f_{v}+f_{v}^{*}}{2}\right)-i\left(\frac{f_{v}-f_{v}^{*}}{2i}\right).

By smoothness, there is a open compact subgroup
U
such that
f_{v}=f_{v}\star\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{U}=\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{U}\star f_{v}
. Then

f_{v}=\frac{1}{4}(f_{v}+\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{U})\star(f_{v}+\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{U})-\frac{1}{4}(f_{v}-\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{U})\star(f_{v}-\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{U}),

a linear combination of functions of the form
g\star g^{*}
, each necessarily trace-positive.

Using
U=K_{v}
gives the required bounds for the second statement.
∎

We can now state the first important technical bound resulting from our methods:

Theorem 9.3.2
.

Fix
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(N)
,
f_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\infty}
, and
\Lambda
as in Section
9.1
.
Then there are constants
A,B,C,D,E
depending only on
G
with
C\geq 1
such that whenever
|\mathfrak{n}|\geq Dq_{S_{1}}^{E\kappa}
, we have

(9.3.2)

|\mathfrak{n}|^{-\dim G}\Gamma_{N}(\mathfrak{n})^{-1}S^{G}_{\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})=\Lambda+O(|\mathfrak{n}|^{-C}q_{S_{1}}^{A+B\kappa}).

For
\Delta\neq\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta}
, the same constants give the upper bound

(9.3.3)

S^{G}_{\Delta}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})=O(|\mathfrak{n}|^{\bar{R}(\Delta)}q_{S_{1}}^{A+B\kappa}),

where the exponent
\bar{R}(\Delta)
is given by

(9.3.4)

\bar{R}((T_{i},d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})_{1\leq i\leq k})=\frac{1}{2}\left(N^{2}+\sum_{i}T_{i}^{2}d_{i}\right).

Proof.

We induct on the
N
such that
G\in\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{ell}}(N)
. For
N=1
,
\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta}
is the only possible shape so (by extreme overkill) this follows from Theorem
9.1.1
.

For the inductive step, we first argue that it suffices to show (
9.3.3
): we start from (
9.3.1
) and take
D
and
E
to be the maximum values over those for all smaller shapes
\Delta\neq\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta}
appearing in the expansion. Then (
9.3.3
) will show that all the corresponding terms are lower order in
|\mathfrak{n}|
than the
S^{G}
-asymptotic from Proposition
9.2.2
. This produces the exact asymptotic (
9.3.2
).

Therefore, let
\Delta=(T_{i},d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})_{1\leq i\leq k}\neq\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta}
have rank
N
. First, we apply Lemma
9.3.1
on each of the finitely many unramified factors of
\varphi_{i,S_{0}}
and
\mathcal{T}_{i}f_{S_{i}}
to write

S^{G}_{\Delta}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})=\sum_{j}\lambda_{j}S^{G}_{\Delta}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n},j})

where the sum of the
|\lambda_{j}|
is
O(C^{|S_{1}|})=O(q_{S_{1}})
and each
f^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n},j}
is trace-positive.

We then apply Proposition
7.3.3
with
S_{s}
the primes that divide
\mathfrak{n}
and
S_{b}=S_{0}
. Over
S_{s}
, let
M=\prod_{i}\mathrm{GL}_{T_{i}}(F_{S_{s}})^{d_{i}}
be the Levi from
7.3.3
and
P
the parabolic defining the constant term. Then (using notation from
7.3.3
to decompose
f_{\mathfrak{n},j}^{\infty}
),

(9.3.5)

\displaystyle|S^{G}_{\Delta}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n},j})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Delta}\prodf\left(S^{T_{i}}_{\Sigma_{\lambda_{i},\eta_{i}}}(((f_{S_{s}})_{M,i}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,S_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{i}f_{S_{1}}\mathbf{1}_{K^{S}})^{T_{i}})\right)^{d_{i}\oplus}

\displaystyle=C_{\Delta}I(\mathfrak{n})\prodf\left(S^{H_{i}}_{\Sigma_{\lambda_{i},\eta_{i}}}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda_{i}}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K^{H_{i}}(\mathfrak{n})}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,S_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{i}f_{S_{1}})\right)^{d_{i}\oplus},

where each
H_{i}=H(\Sigma^{T_{i}}_{\lambda_{i},\eta_{i}})
and the second equality uses Lemma
9.2.1
together with factoring
\vol(K^{M}(\mathfrak{n}))
over places in
S_{s}
.

From Lemma
6.2.3
, we know that
\mathcal{T}f_{S_{1}}\in\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{ur}}(M_{S_{1}})^{\leq\kappa}
and

\|\mathcal{T}f_{S_{1}}\|_{\infty}=\|f_{S_{1}}\|_{\infty}O(q_{v}^{\kappa F}\kappa^{G}).

Therefore, we can use the inductive hypothesis on the
\Sigma_{\lambda_{i},\eta_{i}}
, summing the asymptotics of the
\Lambda
and the error to get that each term in the product (
9.3.5
) is

O\left(|\mathfrak{n}|^{\dim H_{i}}\Gamma_{T_{i}^{(d_{i})}}(\mathfrak{n})q_{S_{1}}^{A^{\prime}+B^{\prime}\kappa}\right)

for some constants
A^{\prime},B^{\prime}
depending on
\Delta
. Note that the positivity condition is guaranteed by the last implication of Proposition
7.3.3
. Summing over
j
, this gives

|S^{G}_{\Delta}(\mathrm{EP}_{\lambda}f^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{n}})|=O\left(\Gamma_{T_{1}^{(d_{1})},\dotsc,T_{k}^{(d_{k})}}(\mathfrak{n})I(\mathfrak{n})|\mathfrak{n}|^{\dim M}q_{S_{1}}^{A^{\prime}+B^{\prime}\kappa}\right),

ignoring all dependence on the
\varphi^{\prime}
.

Recalling that
G_{S_{s}}\simeq\mathrm{GL}_{N,S_{s}}
, we can use the asymptotic for
I(\mathfrak{n})
from
9.2.1
:

I(\mathfrak{n})|\mathfrak{n}|^{\dim M}=O\left(|\mathfrak{n}|^{\dim P}\Gamma_{-1}(\mathfrak{n})^{\sigma(M)}\right).

We finally note that
\Gamma_{T_{1}^{(d_{1})},\dotsc,T_{k}^{(d_{k})}}(\mathfrak{n})\Gamma_{-1}(\mathfrak{n})^{\sigma(M)}\leq 1
and that

\dim P=\bar{R}(\Delta)=\frac{1}{2}\left(N^{2}+\sum_{i}T_{i}^{2}d_{i}\right).

This finishes the bound for
\Delta
and therefore the induction.
∎

We emphasize that the value

\bar{R}(\Delta)
, which is the dimension of the parabolic attached to the partition

(T_{1}^{(d_{1})},\dotsc,T_{k}^{(d_{k})}),

is an approximate upper bound to what we expect to be
the true growth rate attached to
\Delta
. It is
maximized at
\bar{R}(\Sigma_{\lambda,\eta})=\dim G
where it is exact.

9.4.
Improving the Bound

We can prove a slightly tighter bound
R(\Delta)
. Instead of applying
7.3.3
directly as in the proof of
9.3.2
, we will apply
7.2.2
and then use a different method to bound the factors for simple blocks
(T_{i},d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})
with small
T_{i}
. For large
T_{i}
, we will proceed as before using the arguments of §
7.3
. This argument is nothing more than a rephrasing in our context of the key technical trick that makes the bounds in
[
MS19
]
work.

9.4.1.
Terms with
T_{i}=1

In this case we actually prove a stronger exact formula:

Lemma 9.4.1
.

When
T_{i}=1
, the terms for the factors
S_{(T_{i},d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})}
coming from the use of Corollary
7.2.2
implicit in (
9.3.5
) satisfy the bound:

\displaystyle S^{H^{\prime}_{i}}_{(1,d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})}(

\displaystyle EP_{\lambda_{i}[d_{i}]}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K^{H^{\prime}_{i}}(\mathfrak{n})}\varphi_{i,S_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{i}f_{S_{1}})

\displaystyle=|\mathfrak{n}|\Gamma_{1}(\mathfrak{n}_{i})\frac{\vol(H^{\prime}_{i}(F)^{\mathrm{ab}}\backslash H^{\prime}_{i}(\mathbb{A})^{\mathrm{ab}})}{\vol(K^{(H^{\prime}_{i})^{\mathrm{ab}},S})\vol((H^{\prime}_{i,\infty})^{\mathrm{ab}})}\int_{H^{\prime}_{i,\mathrm{der},S_{1},S_{0}}}\mathcal{T}_{i}f_{S_{1}}\varphi_{i,S_{0}}(h)\,dh

\displaystyle=O(|\mathfrak{n}|\Gamma_{1}(\mathfrak{n})q_{S_{1}}^{A+B\kappa})

as long as
|\mathfrak{n}|>Dq_{S_{1}}^{E\kappa}
.

Proof.

By Corollary
8.1.2
,

S^{H^{\prime}_{i}}_{(1,d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})}(EP_{\lambda_{i}[d_{i}]}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K^{H^{\prime}_{i}}(\mathfrak{n})}\varphi_{i,S_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{i}f_{S_{1}})\\
=\frac{\vol(H^{\prime}_{i}(F)^{\mathrm{ab}}\backslash H^{\prime}_{i}(\mathbb{A})^{\mathrm{ab}})}{\vol((H^{\prime}_{i,\infty})^{\mathrm{ab}})}\sum_{\gamma\in H^{\prime}_{i}(F)^{\mathrm{ab}}}(\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K^{H^{\prime}_{i}}(\mathfrak{n})})^{\mathrm{ab}}(\gamma)(\mathcal{T}_{i}f_{S_{1}})^{\mathrm{ab}}(\gamma)\varphi_{i,S_{0}}^{\mathrm{ab}}(\gamma)\xi^{-1}_{i}(\gamma)

where
\xi_{i}
is the character of
H^{\prime}_{i}
associated to infinitesimal character
\lambda_{i}[d_{i}]
.

At places dividing
\mathfrak{n}_{i}
we know that
H_{i}
is a general linear group, so we compute

(\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K^{H^{\prime}_{i}}(\mathfrak{n})})^{\mathrm{ab}}=|\mathfrak{n}|\Gamma_{1}(\mathfrak{n})\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K^{(H^{\prime}_{i})^{\mathrm{ab}}}(\mathfrak{n})}

using standard formulas for
|\mathrm{SL}_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{F}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{n})|
.
Next,
H^{\prime}_{i}(F)^{\mathrm{ab}}\subseteq(H^{\prime})_{i}^{\mathrm{ab}}(F)
so a trivial case of Lemma 8.4 of
[
ST16
]
applied to
(H^{\prime}_{i})^{\mathrm{ab}}
gives
D_{i}
and
E_{i}
such that whenever
|\mathfrak{n}|\geq D_{i}q_{S_{1}}^{E_{i}\kappa}
, all terms in the sum vanish except for
\gamma=1
. This finishes the argument.
∎

9.4.2.
Terms with
T_{i}=2

Lemma 9.4.2
.

When
T_{i}=2
, the terms for factors
S_{(T_{i},d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})}
coming from the use of Corollary
7.2.2
implicit in (
9.3.5
) satisfy the bound:

S^{H^{\prime}_{i}}_{(2,d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})}(EP_{\lambda_{i}[d_{i}]}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K^{H^{\prime}_{i}}(\mathfrak{n})}\varphi_{i,S_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{i}f_{S_{1}})\\
=O(|\mathfrak{n}|^{2d_{i}(d_{i}-1)+d_{i}+3}\Gamma_{-1^{(2d_{i}-2)},2}(\mathfrak{n})q_{S_{1}}^{A+B\kappa}).

Proof.

We use Proposition
7.3.2
instead of Proposition
7.3.1
. Bound
\tr_{\pi_{S_{0}}}(\varphi_{i,S_{0}})
by a constant through Bernstein admissibility as in Lemma
6.3.7
. Next, the Satake eigenvalues of unirreps of
\mathrm{GL}_{N}
always have their
|\log_{q_{v}}(\cdot)|
bounded by
(N-1)/2
by the main result of
[
Tad86
]
(this is the value achieved by the trivial representation). Therefore, arguments as in
[
Dal22
, §5.5]
show that

\tr_{\pi_{S_{1}}}(\mathcal{T}_{i}f_{S_{1}})=O(q_{S_{1}}^{A+B\kappa})

for all unirreps
\pi_{S_{1}}
of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
at
S_{1}
(the number of factorizable summands of
\mathcal{T}f_{S_{1}}
is bounded similarly).

Finally, for any irrep
\pi^{S}
we have, as in the proof of Lemma 5.2 in
[
MS19
]
,

\tr_{\pi^{S}}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K^{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}(\mathfrak{n})}\leq\Gamma_{-1}(\mathfrak{n})|\mathfrak{n}|.

Applying
7.3.2
and all the above bounds (and changing
A,B)
:

S^{H^{\prime}_{i}}_{(2,d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})}(EP_{\lambda_{i}[d_{i}]}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K^{H^{\prime}_{i}}(\mathfrak{n})}\varphi_{i,S_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{i}f_{S_{1}})\\
=O(|\mathfrak{n}|^{d-1}\Gamma_{-1}(\mathfrak{n})^{d_{i}-1}q_{S_{1}}^{A+B\kappa})\\
\times|\mathfrak{n}|^{2d_{i}(d_{i}-1)}\Gamma_{-1}(\mathfrak{n})^{d_{i}-1}S^{H_{i}}_{\Sigma_{\lambda_{i},\eta_{i}}}(EP_{\lambda_{i}}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K^{H_{i}}(\mathfrak{n})}\varphi_{i,S_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{i}f_{S_{1}}).

Where the factors on the second line outside the big-
O
come from taking the constant term of
\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{K^{H}(\mathfrak{n}_{i})}
. Substituting in Theorem
9.3.2
produces the result noting that
H_{i}=H(2,1,\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{ell}}(2)
.
∎

9.4.3.
Terms with
T_{i}=3

Lemma 9.4.3
.

When
T_{i}=3
, the terms for factors
S_{(T_{i},d_{i},\lambda_{i},\eta_{i})}
coming from the use of Corollary
7.2.2
implicit in (
9.3.5
) satisfy that for all
\epsilon>0
:
